PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended June 80, 1899. 26
202 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. Larmu- , NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 37. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. SOUTH CHANNEL ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. ALTERATION IN NATURE OF THE TUNGsHA LIGHT-VESSEL. NoTIC is hereby given that the Tungsha light-vessel Sewchwang has been withdrawn and replaced by a new light-vessel having the following characteristics:- The light is catoptric, group-flashing, showing three white flashes in quick succession every 45 seconds. It is elevated 38 feet above the sea, and in clear weather should be visible for a distance of ii miles. A small white light will be exhibited from the forestay, at a height of 6 feet above the rail, for the purpose-of showing in what direction the vessel is riding. The light-vessel is painted red, with the word &quot; TUNGHA &quot; in white letters on each side, and she has one mast surmounted by a black ball. During thick or foggy weather a first class double-note fog siren, giving a low and a high note every I( minutes, will be sounded. In the event of the fog siren being disabled, a gong will be sounded at intervals of iI minutes. If the light-vessel be driven from her proper position to one where she is of no use as a guide to shipping, the usual light will not be shown, but a fixed red light will be exhibited at each end of the vessel; the ball will be struck as soon as possible, and till struck it will be, surmounted. by a red flag. SHANGHAI, 4th April 1899.
1899.] NOTIFICATIONS. 203 No. 338. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. ALTERATION IN CHARACTER OF SHAWEISHAN LIGHT. NoTicz is hereby given that the character of the light at Shaweishan has been changed. The new light is dioptric, occulting, of the first order, showing a fixed white light varied by an eclipse every 15 seconds. It is elevated 229 feet above the level of the sea, and in clear weather should be visible for a distance of 22 nautical miles. SHANGHAI, 2oth April 1899. No. 339. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. &quot;KIUTOAN&quot; LIGHT-VESSEL. ALtERATION IN CHARACTER OF LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the character of the light of the Kitoan light-vessel has been changed. The new light is catoptric, revolving, showing a white flash every J minute. It is elevated 35 feet above the sea, and should be visible in clear weather for a distance of i i nautical miles. SHANGHAI, 12th May 1899. No. 340. FOOCHOW DISTRICT. ENTRANCE TO MIN RIVER. BANK TO SOUTHwARD OF SOUTH POINT OF SHARP PEAK ISLAND. NOTICE is hereby given that a bank of small extent, with 8 feet of water on it at low water of spring tides, exists about 2 cables S. 4 W. from the south point of Sharp Peak Island. To avoid this bank vessels entering the Min River should, after crossing the Inner Bar, bring the south end of West Brother just open of the south end of East Brother, and keep them so until Woga Point and High Sharp Peak are in line. - These latter two marks kept in line on a N.W. course will lead into the deep water to the southward of Woga Hill. SHAwNA, 2ot May -1899.
204 CUSTOMS GAZTTE. [APW-JUNE, 1899.] No. 841. SWATOW DISTRICT. ALTERATION -IN CHARACTER OF LAMOCKS LIGHT. NoTic is hereby given that the character of the high light at the Lamocks has been changed. The new light is dioptrie, occulting, of the first order, showing a fixed White light varied by an eclipse every 20 seconds. It is elevated 241 feet above the level of the sea, and in clear weather should be visible for a distance of 22 nautical miles. SHAnGHAI, 13th JU%e 1899.

APPENDIX I. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. APRILJUNE 1899.
220 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, (Received i9th July 1899.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, IrTR JULY 1899. SIR, L-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the second quarter of the year 1899, April-June. b. The Custom House (:L ft ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.7T 30 and Hk.2k 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (g * ) Changehow (R f), Fotochow ( 0 N), and Kowloon (j g) stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. 1899.] LAPPA. 231 (Received i8th Jdy 1899.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 12TH JULY 18&amp;lt;9. SIR, L--a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the second quarter of the year 1899, April-June. b. The Custom House (a t U) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.2h 3o and Hk.ft 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (Aj i N) and Chienshan (&quot;gj 0I) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (6 f), Kuttai (L *), and Kwanchiap ( ) sub-stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. MOVEMENTS OF VESSELS.-The number and movements of vessels, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Tables:- I. MALOWCHOW. JUNKS. APRIL. MAY. JUNE. TOTAL.
